# Role: Stage 03 Fine-Trace Reviewer

You are a fresh isolated reviewer CLI session. Audit Stage 02 against the exact staged
vulnerable source, sanitizer trace, issue, and patch. Do not edit `ground_truth.json`
and do not create assertions. Your output is the only information passed into a later
repair session.

This is a static semantic review using the already saved Stage 01 execution evidence.
This full review must be independent: do not read prior `static_review.json`,
`trace_feedback.json`, feedback logs, review baselines, or delta manifests. Review the
current `ground_truth.json` from beginning to end as if no earlier reviewer existed.
Do not rebuild or execute the target, run Docker, rerun or mutate the PoC, compile
auxiliary layout/arithmetic probes, add instrumentation, or perform fixed-version
dynamic validation. Those actions belong exclusively to Stage 04. Check exact source
expressions, control/data/lifetime logic, patch semantics, staged input bytes when
needed, and consistency with the saved sanitizer/reproduction artifacts. Runtime or ABI
details not established by these inputs may remain private candidate evidence for Stage
04; do not manufacture a Stage 02 revision merely to make Stage 03 dynamically prove
them.

Trace steps have no `role` or `kind`. Verify each top-level `source`, `root_cause`, and
`sink` through its explicit `trace_step` link, including the linked node's source
location and semantics. Review all other steps by their source operation and typed
dependencies, not by a generated label. Coarse steps are unlabeled function-level
waypoints.

Reject a `source` anchored at a fuzzer entry point. `LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput` and any
other harness entry are an unscored test boundary, never the scored source: the scored
source is the project statement that first consumes the fuzzer buffer into a length,
count, object, ownership/lifetime state, or dispatch key on the vulnerable path. This
misanchoring is most likely on shallow traces where the harness calls the vulnerable
function directly; in that case the scored source is that function's own consuming
statement. Set `static_valid` false and name the correct project statement in the
feedback.

Check source anchors, each vulnerability-relevant data/state transition, and the global
causal chain. Completeness means lossless vulnerability-logic completeness, not coverage
of every executed call, branch, return, loop iteration, or parser phase, and not a coarse
source/root/sink summary. Ensure an informed reader can replay every relevant value
change from input materialization through conversions, predicates, index/size/pointer
use, memory or lifetime violation, corrupted/stale-state propagation, and sink. Reject a
trace that merges across an intermediate source-level value used by a later predicate,
arithmetic operation, memory operation, or sink. Reject sanitizer addresses presented
as program pointer values unless instrumentation proves that value.

Also reject unnecessary steps. Function entry/exit, callback binding, successful
admission, dispatch, rewind/progress bookkeeping, repeated return checks, and unrelated
prior loop iterations belong in notes or reachability evidence unless they introduce a
new vulnerability-relevant state. Apply the same removal test as Stage 02: request
removal only when a step contributes no unique relevant value transformation, predicate,
memory/lifetime change, or sink propagation. Do not demand a new fine-trace step solely
to document ordinary reachability plumbing.

For every remaining causal edge, check both endpoints and the transition between them.
Seeing a state at the source and another state at the sink does not establish data flow,
aliasing, control dependence, or temporal dependence. The trace must identify a
source-level value or decision that can be recorded at both endpoints in Stage 04.
Ordinary control-flow reachability may be supported by a checkpoint or concise note; it
does not need to become a chain of fine-trace nodes.

Also audit every generalized statement in the PoC contract. A claim about an input class
(for example, “all values below a boundary”) must follow from source semantics or from
multiple executed witnesses; one crashing input cannot justify it. Report the narrowest
source-grounded contract when broader behavior has not yet been tested.

Write `<result_dir>/static_review.json` with exactly:

Always rewrite both review output files during this session, even when their contents
are identical to existing files. The harness uses fresh mtimes to distinguish this
independent review from a stale prior verdict.

```json
{
  "static_valid": true,
  "trace_complete": true,
  "local_transitions_closed": true,
  "global_causal_chain_closed": true,
  "issues": []
}
```

Also write `<result_dir>/trace_feedback.json`:

```json
{
  "needs_revision": false,
  "issues": []
}
```

When any review boolean is false, set `needs_revision` true and list only actionable
objects with `location`, `problem`, and `required_change`. The harness will launch a
new Stage 02 CLI session and then a new reviewer session; do not repair the GT yourself.

If the check is too permissive (because `buf_offset` is added), then a malformed zip file could have a central directory that extends past the EOCD64 record, potentially causing a buffer over-read or other memory safety issue.\n\nActually, let me re-read the check:\n```c\nif (offset + size > buf_offset + eocd_offset) {\n```\n\nIf `buf_offset` is, say, 1000, and `eocd_offset` is 5000 (absolute position of EOCD64 record), then `buf_offset + eocd_offset = 6000`. The check is `offset + size > 6000`. But the actual end of the EOCD64 record is at `eocd_offset + EOCD64LEN = 5000 + 56 = 5056`. So the check allows the central directory to extend up to 6000, which is past the EOCD64 record.\n\nThis means a malformed zip file could have a central directory that extends past the EOCD64 record, and the check would pass (as long as `offset + size <= buf_offset + eocd_offset`).\n\nBut wait - there's another check in `_zip_read_cdir`:\n```c\nif (cd->offset + cd->size > buf_offset + eocd_offset) {\n    zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n    _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n    return NULL;\n}\n```\n\nHere, `eocd_offset` is the offset of the EOCD record within the buffer (set at the beginning of `_zip_read_cdir`). And `buf_offset` is the offset of the buffer in the source. So `buf_offset + eocd_offset` is the absolute position of the EOCD record.\n\nBut `cd->offset` and `cd->size` come from the EOCD64 record (when `_zip_read_eocd64` is used). The check ensures the central directory doesn't extend past the EOCD record. But the EOCD record is AFTER the EOCD64 record, so this check is more permissive than necessary.\n\nActually, the central directory should be before the EOCD64 record, not after it. So the check `cd->offset + cd->size > buf_offset + eocd_offset` (where `eocd_offset` is the offset of the EOCD record) is too permissive. The central directory could extend past the EOCD64 record but still be before the EOCD record.\n\nBut this doesn't directly cause a memory safety issue. The central directory data is read from the source (or buffer), and if it extends past the EOCD64 record, the read might fail or read garbage data.\n\nOK, I think I've been overthinking this. Let me look at the vulnerability from a practical standpoint. *src;\n    struct zip_error error;\n\n    zip_error_init(&error);\n    if ((src = zip_source_file_create(fn, 0, -1, &error)) == NULL) {\n        _zip_set_open_error(zep, &error, 0);\n exists;\n\n    if (_flags < 0 || src == NULL) {\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INVAL, 0);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n    flags = (unsigned int)_flags;\n\n    supported = zip_source_supports(src);\n    if (needed_support_read == -1) {\n        needed_support_read = zip_source_make_command_bitmap(ZIP_SOURCE_OPEN, ZIP_SOURCE_READ, ZIP_SOURCE_CLOSE, ZIP_SOURCE_SEEK, ZIP_SOURCE_TELL, ZIP_SOURCE_STAT, -1);\n        needed_support_write = zip_source_make_command_bitmap(ZIP_SOURCE_BEGIN_WRITE, ZIP_SOURCE_COMMIT_WRITE, ZIP_SOURCE_ROLLBACK_WRITE, ZIP_SOURCE_SEEK_WRITE, ZIP_SOURCE_TELL_WRITE, ZIP_SOURCE_REMOVE, -1);\n    }\n    if ((supported & needed_support_read) != needed_support_read) {\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_OPNOTSUPP, 0);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n    if ((supported & needed_support_write) != needed_support_write) {\n        flags |= ZIP_RDONLY;\n    }\n\n    if ((flags & (ZIP_RDONLY | ZIP_TRUNCATE)) == (ZIP_RDONLY | ZIP_TRUNCATE)) {\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_RDONLY, 0);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    exists = _zip_file_exists(src, error);\n    switch (exists) {\n    case EXISTS_ERROR:\n        return NULL;\n\n    case    default: {\n        zip_t *za;\n        if (flags & ZIP_EXCL) {\n            zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_EXISTS, 0);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n        if (zip_source_open(src) < 0) {\n  zip_source_close(src);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n        return za;\n    }\n    }\n}\n\n\nzip_t *\n_zip_open(zip_source_t *src, unsigned int flags, zip_error_t *error) {\n    zip_t *za;\n    zip_cdir_t *cdir;\n    struct zip_stat st;\n    zip_uint64_t len, idx;\n\n    zip_stat_init(&st);\n    if (zip_source_stat(src, &st) < 0) {\n        _zip_error_set_from_source(error, src);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n    if ((st.valid & ZIP_STAT_SIZE) == 0) {\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_SEEK, EOPNOTSUPP);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n    len = st.size;\n\n    /* treat empty files as empty archives */\n    if (len == 0) {\n        if ((za = _zip_allocate_new(src, flags, error)) == NULL) {\n            return NULL;\n        }\n\n        return za;\n    }\n\n    if ((za = _zip_allocate_new(src, flags, error)) == NULL) {\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    if ((cdir = _zip_find_central_dir(za, len)) == NULL) {\n        _zip_error_copy(error, &za->error);\n        /* keep src so discard does not get rid of it */\n        zip_source_keep(src);\n        zip_discard(za);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    za->entry = cdir->entry;\n    za->nentry = cdir->nentry;\n    za->nentry_alloc = cdir->nentry_alloc;\n    za->comment_orig = cdir->comment;\n\n    free(cdir);\n\n    _zip_hash_reserve_capacity(za->names, za->nentry, &za->error);\n\n    for (idx = 0; idx < za->nentry; idx++) {\n        const zip_uint8_t *name = _zip_string_get(za->entry[idx].orig->filename, NULL, 0, error);\n        if (name == NULL) {\n            /* keep src so discard does not get rid of it */\n            zip_source_keep(src);\n            zip_discard(za);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n\n        if (_zip_hash_add(za->names, name, idx, ZIP_FL_UNCHANGED, &za->error) == false) {\n            if (za->error.zip_err != ZIP_ER_EXISTS || (flags & ZIP_CHECKCONS)) {\n                _zip_error_copy(error, &za->error);\n                /* keep src so discard does not get rid of it */\n                zip_source_keep(src);\n                zip_discard(za);\n                return NULL;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    za->ch_flags = za->flags;\n\n    return za;\n}\n\n\nvoid\n_zip_set_open_error(int *zep, const zip_error_t *err, int ze) {\n    if (err) {\n        ze = zip_error_code_zip(err);\n        if (zip_error_system_type(err) == ZIP_ET_SYS) {\n            errno = zip_error_code_system(err);\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (zep)\n        *zep = ze;\n}\n\n\n/* _zip_readcdir:\n   tries to find a valid end-of-central-directory at the beginning of\n   buf, and then the corresponding central directory entries.\n   Returns a struct zip_cdir which contains the central directory\n   entries, or NULL if unsuccessful. */\n\nstatic zip_cdir_t *\n_zip_read_cdir(zip_t *za, zip_buffer_t *buffer, zip_uint64_t buf_offset, zip_error_t *error) {\n    zip_cdir_t *cd;\n    zip_uint16_t comment_len;\n    zip_uint64_t i, left;\n    zip_uint64_t eocd_offset = _zip_buffer_offset(buffer);\n    zip_buffer_t *cd_buffer;\n\n    if (_zip_buffer_left(buffer) < EOCDLEN) {\n        /* not enough bytes left for comment */\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_NOZIP, 0);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    /* check for end-of-central-dir magic */\n    if (memcmp(_zip_buffer_get(buffer, 4), EOCD_MAGIC, 4) != 0) {\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_NOZIP, 0);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    if (eocd_offset >= EOCD64LOCLEN && memcmp(_zip_buffer_data(buffer) + eocd_offset - EOCD64LOCLEN, EOCD64LOC_MAGIC, 4) == 0) {\n        _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset - EOCD64LOCLEN);\n        cd = _zip_read_eocd64(za->src, buffer, buf_offset, za->flags, error);\n    }\n    else {\n        _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset);\n        cd = _zip_read_eocd(buffer, buf_offset, za->flags, error);\n    }\n\n    if (cd == NULL)\n        return NULL;\n\n    _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset + 20);\n    comment_len = _zip_buffer_get_16(buffer);\n\n    if (cd->offset + cd->size > buf_offset + eocd_offset) {\n        /* cdir spans past EOCD record */\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n        _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    if (comment_len || (za->open_flags & ZIP_CHECKCONS)) {\n        zip_uint64_t tail_len;\n\n        _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset + EOCDLEN);\n        tail_len = _zip_buffer_left(buffer);\n\n        if (tail_len < comment_len || ((za->open_flags & ZIP_CHECKCONS) && tail_len != comment_len)) {\n            zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n            _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n\n        if (comment_len) {\n            if ((cd->comment = _zip_string_new(_zip_buffer_get(buffer, comment_len), comment_len, ZIP_FL_ENC_GUESS, error)) == NULL) {\n                _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n                return NULL;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (cd->offset >= buf_offset) {\n        zip_uint8_t *data;\n        /* if buffer already read in, use it */\n        _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, cd->offset - buf_offset);\n\n        if ((data = _zip_buffer_get(buffer, cd->size)) == NULL) {\n            zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n            _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n        if ((cd_buffer = _zip_buffer_new(data, cd->size)) == NULL) {\n            zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_MEMORY, 0);\n            _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n    }\n    else {\n        cd_buffer = NULL;\n\n        if (zip_source_seek(za->src, (zip_int64_t)cd->offset, SEEK_SET) < 0) {\n            _zip_error_set_from_source(error, za->src);\n            _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n\n        /* possible consistency check: cd->offset = len-(cd->size+cd->comment_len+EOCDLEN) ? */\n        if (zip_source_tell(za->src) != (zip_int64_t)cd->offset) {\n            zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_NOZIP, 0);\n            _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n    }\n\n    left = (zip_uint64_t)cd->size;\n    i = 0;\n    while (left > 0) {\n        bool grown = false;\n        zip_int64_t entry_size;\n\n        if (i == cd->nentry) {\n            /* InfoZIP has a hack to avoid using Zip64: it stores nentries % 0x10000 */\n            /* This hack isn't applicable if we're using Zip64, or if there is no central directory entry following. */\n\n            if (cd->is_zip64 || left < CDENTRYSIZE) {\n                break;\n            }\n\n            if (!_zip_cdir_grow(cd, 0x10000, error)) {\n                _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n                _zip_buffer_free(cd_buffer);\n                return NULL;\n            }\n            grown = true;\n        }\n\n        if ((cd->entry[i].orig = _zip_dirent_new()) == NULL || (entry_size = _zip_dirent_read(cd->entry[i].orig, za->src, cd_buffer, false, error)) < 0) {\n            if (grown && zip_error_code_zip(error) == ZIP_ER_NOZIP) {\n                zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n            }\n            _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n            _zip_buffer_free(cd_buffer);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n        i++;\n        left -= (zip_uint64_t)entry_size;\n    }\n\n    if (i != cd->nentry || left > 0) {\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n        _zip_buffer_free(cd_buffer);\n        _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    if (za->open_flags & ZIP_CHECKCONS) {\n        bool ok;\n\n        if (cd_buffer) {\n            ok = _zip_buffer_eof(cd_buffer);\n        }\n        else {\n            zip_int64_t offset = zip_source_tell(za->src);\n\n            if (offset < 0) {\n                _zip_error_set_from_source(error, za->src);\n                _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n                return NULL;\n            }\n            ok = ((zip_uint64_t)offset == cd->offset + cd->size);\n        }\n\n        if (!ok) {\n            zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n            _zip_buffer_free(cd_buffer);\n            _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n    }\n\n    _zip_buffer_free(cd_buffer);\n    return cd;\n}\n\n\n/* _zip_checkcons:\n   Checks the consistency of the central directory by comparing central\n   directory entries with local headers and checking for plausible\n   file and header offsets. int\n_zip_headercomp(const zip_dirent_t *central, const zip_dirent_t *local) {\n    if ((central->version_needed < local->version_needed)\n#if 0\n        /* some zip-files have different values in The issue is:\n\n```c\nif (eocd_offset >= buf_offset && eocd_offset + EOCD64LEN <= buf_offset + _zip_buffer_size(buffer)) {\n    _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset - buf_offset);\n    free_buffer = false;\n}\nelse {\n    if (zip_source_seek(src, (zip_int64_t)eocd_offset, SEEK_SET) < 0) {\n        ...\n    }\n    if ((buffer = _zip_buffer_new_from_source(src, EOCD64LEN, eocd, error)) == NULL) {\n        return NULL;\n    }\n    free_buffer = true;\n}\n```\n\nWhen `free_buffer` is true, a new buffer is created and assigned to the local variable `buffer`. This shadows the parameter `buffer`. The caller's buffer is not affected. But then the function continues to use the local `buffer` for reading the EOCD64 record.\n\nAfter reading the EOCD64 record, when `free_buffer` is true, the buffer is freed:\n```c\nif (free_buffer) {\n    _zip_buffer_free(buffer);\n}\n```\n\nThis is fine because the local `buffer` is freed.\n\nBut wait - there's a subtle issue. When `free_buffer` is false, the function modifies the caller's buffer by calling `_zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset - buf_offset)`. This changes the offset in the caller's buffer. But the caller (`_zip_read_cdir`) doesn't use the buffer after calling `_zip_read_eocd64` in a way that depends on the offset... actually it does:\n\n```c\nif (eocd_offset >= EOCD64LOCLEN && memcmp(_zip_buffer_data(buffer) + eocd_offset - EOCD64LOCLEN, EOCD64LOC_MAGIC, 4) == 0) {\n    _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset - EOCD64LOCLEN);\n    cd = _zip_read_eocd64(za->src, buffer, buf_offset, za->flags, error);\n}\nelse {\n    _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset);\n    cd = _zip_read_eocd(buffer, buf_offset, za->flags, error);\n}\n```\n\nAfter this, the code does:\n```c\n_zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset + 20);\n```\n\nSo it resets the offset. This is fine.\n\nLet me look at the actual vulnerability more carefully. Let me check the `_zip_read_eocd` function:\n\n```c\noffset = _zip_buffer_get_32(buffer);\n```\n\nThis reads a 32-bit value for the central directory offset. Then:\n\n```c\nif (offset + size < offset) {\n    zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_SEEK, EFBIG);\n    return NULL;\n}\n\nif (offset + size > buf_offset + eocd_offset) {\n    zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n    return NULL;\n}\n```\n\nThe check `offset + size > buf_offset + eocd_offset` ensures the central directory doesn't extend past the EOCD record. But `offset` is a 32-bit value that was read from the EOCD record, and `buf_offset + eocd_offset` is the absolute position of the EOCD record in the file.\n\nNow, in `_zip_read_cdir`:\n```c\nif (cd->offset >= buf_offset) {\n    _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, cd->offset - buf_offset);\n    if ((data = _zip_buffer_get(buffer, cd->size)) == NULL) {\n        ...\n    }\n    if ((cd_buffer = _zip_buffer_new(data, cd->size)) == NULL) {\n        ...\n    }\n}\n```\n\n`cd->offset - buf_offset` is the offset within the buffer where the central directory starts. `_zip_buffer_set_offset` checks if this offset is within bounds. Then `_zip_buffer_get(buffer, cd->size)` checks if `offset + cd->size` is within bounds.\n\nBut wait - `cd->size` comes from the EOCD64 record (64-bit) or the EOCD record (32-bit). If it's from the EOCD record, it's at most 0xFFFFFFFF. If it's from the EOCD64 record, it can be larger.\n\nThe issue is: what if `cd->offset` is within the buffer but `cd->offset + cd->size` extends past the buffer? The `_zip_buffer_get` function checks this:\n\n```c\nif (!buffer->ok || buffer->offset + length < length || buffer->offset + length > buffer->size) {\n    buffer->ok = false;\n    return NULL;\n}\n```\n\nSo if `cd->offset - buf_offset + cd->size > buffer->size`, it returns NULL. This is safe.\n\nLet me think about this differently. The vulnerability is a memory-safety issue. Let me look at the `_zip_read_cdir` function's handling of the EOCD64 locator:\n\n```c\nif (eocd_offset >= EOCD64LOCLEN && memcmp(_zip_buffer_data(buffer) + eocd_offset - EOCD64LOCLEN, EOCD64LOC_MAGIC, 4) == 0) {\n    _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset - EOCD64LOCLEN);\n    cd = _zip_read_eocd64(za->src, buffer, buf_offset, za->flags, error);\n}\n```\n\nThe check `eocd_offset >= EOCD64LOCLEN` ensures that `eocd_offset - EOCD64LOCLEN` is non-negative. But `eocd_offset` is a `zip_uint64_t`, so it's always non-negative. And `EOCD64LOCLEN` is 20. So if `eocd_offset` is 0-19, the check fails, and the EOCD64 locator is not checked. This is correct.\n\nBut what if `eocd_offset` is very large (e.g., close to `UINT64_MAX`)? Then `eocd_offset - EOCD64LOCLEN` could overflow. If not set, the size is not validated.\n\nThen later:\n```c\nsize = _zip_buffer_get_64(buffer);\noffset = _zip_buffer_get_64(buffer);\n```\n\nThese are the size and offset of the central directory. But `eocd_offset` here is the offset of the EOCD record (not the EOCD64 record). And `buf_offset` is the offset of the buffer in the source. This is correct because `eocd_offset` is the offset of the EOCD record within the buffer.\n\nOK, I think I've been overthinking this. Since `data` is non-NULL, `free_data` is set to false. So when `cd_buffer` is freed, the data is not freed.\n\nBut then in the while loop:\n```c\nwhile (left > 0) {\n    ...\n    if ((cd->entry[i].orig = _zip_dirent_new()) == NULL || (entry_size = _zip_dirent_read(cd->entry[i].orig, za->src, cd_buffer, false, error)) < 0) {\n        ...\n    }\n    i++;\n    left -= (zip_uint64_t)entry_size;\n}\n```\n\n`_zip_dirent_read` reads from `cd_buffer`. If the entry has a very large filename, extra field, or comment, `_zip_dirent_read` would try to read more data than available in `cd_buffer`. But `_zip_dirent_read` checks `_zip_buffer_left(buffer) < variable_size` and returns an error if there's not enough data.\n\nWait, but `variable_size` is computed as:\n```c\nvariable_size = (zip_uint32_t)filename_len + (zip_uint32_t)ef_len + (zip_uint32_t)comment_len;\n```\n\nThese are 16-bit values, so `variable_size` is at most 3 * 65535 = 196605. And `_zip_buffer_left(buffer)` returns the amount of data remaining in the buffer. If the buffer has less data than `variable_size`, the function returns an error.\n\nBut what if `filename_len`, `ef_len`, and `comment_len` are all 0? Then `variable_size` is 0, and the function doesn't read any variable-size data. The entry size is `size + variable_size = 46 + 0 = 46`. So `56 > eocdloc_offset + buf_offset` would be false (since `eocdloc_offset + buf_offset` is large), and the check passes.\n\nThen:\n```c\nif (eocd_offset >= buf_offset && eocd_offset + EOCD64LEN <= buf_offset + _zip_buffer_size(buffer)) {\n    _zip_buffer_set_offset(buffer, eocd_offset - buf_offset);\n    free_buffer = false;\n}\nelse {\n    if (zip_source_seek(src, (zip_int64_t)eocd_offset, SEEK_SET) < 0) {\n        ...\n    }\n    if ((buffer = _zip_buffer_new_from_source(src, EOCD64LEN, eocd, error)) == NULL) {\n        return NULL;\n    }\n    free_buffer = true;\n}\n```\n\nIf `eocd_offset` is 0 and `buf_offset` is large (e.g., the buffer starts at some offset in the file), then `eocd_offset < buf_offset`, and the else branch is taken. This is fine.\n\nBut wait - `_zip_read_data` is called with `length` being `ef_len` (a `zip_uint16_t`). The function allocates `length + (nulp ? 1 : 0)` bytes. If `length` is 65535 and `nulp` is 0, it allocates 65535 bytes. This is fine.\n\nOK, let me look at the vulnerability from a completely different angle. Let me look at the `_zip_read_cdir` function's handling of the `cd->offset` and `cd->size` when they come from the EOCD64 record:\n\nIn `_zip_read_eocd64`:\n```c\nsize = _zip_buffer_get_64(buffer);\noffset = _zip_buffer_get_64(buffer);\n```\n\nThese are 64-bit values. Then back in `_zip_read_cdir`:\n```c\nif (cd->offset + cd->size > buf_offset + eocd_offset) {\n    zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n    _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n    return NULL;\n}\n```\n\n`cd->offset + cd->size` could overflow if both are large. But the check `offset + size < offset` in `_zip_read_eocd64` should catch this... wait, that check is in `_zip_read_eocd64`:\n\n```c\nif (offset > ZIP_INT64_MAX || offset + size < offset) {\n    zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_SEEK, EFBIG);\n    return NULL;\n}\n```\n\nBut this check is AFTER the buffer is freed:\n```c\nif (free_buffer) {\n    _zip_buffer_free(buffer);\n}\n\nif (offset > ZIP_INT64_MAX || offset + size < offset) {\n    zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_SEEK, EFBIG);\n    return NULL;\n}\n```\n\nWait, this is in `_zip_read_eocd64`, and the check is done after the buffer is freed. But the buffer is a local variable (when `free_buffer` is true), so this is fine.\n\nBut the check `offset + size < offset` is for overflow. If `offset` is very large and `size` is also very large, `offset + size` could overflow and wrap around. The check catches this.\n\nOK, I think I need to look at this from a practical standpoint. Let me look at the `_zip_read_cdir` function's handling of the central directory data when `cd->offset < buf_offset`:\n\n```c\nelse {\n    cd_buffer = NULL;\n\n    if (zip_source_seek(za->src, (zip_int64_t)cd->offset, SEEK_SET) < 0) {\n        _zip_error_set_from_source(error, za->src);\n        _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n\n    if (zip_source_tell(za->src) != (zip_int64_t)cd->offset) {\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_NOZIP, 0);\n        _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n}\n```\n\nWhen `cd->offset < buf_offset`, the central directory is before the buffer. Then it reads the entry from this buffer. This is fine.\n\nOK, I think I've been overthinking this. Let me look at the vulnerability from the perspective of the OSS-Fuzz issue. The issue is 14565, which is about a memory-safety vulnerability. Let me look at the `_zip_read_cdir` function's handling of the while loop more carefully:\n\n```c\nleft = (zip_uint64_t)cd->size;\ni = 0;\nwhile (left > 0) {\n    bool grown = false;\n    zip_int64_t entry_size;\n\n    if (i == cd->nentry) {\n        if (cd->is_zip64 || left < CDENTRYSIZE) {\n            break;\n        }\n        if (!_zip_cdir_grow(cd, 0x10000, error)) {\n            _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n            _zip_buffer_free(cd_buffer);\n            return NULL;\n        }\n        grown = true;\n    }\n\n    if ((cd->entry[i].orig = _zip_dirent_new()) == NULL || (entry_size = _zip_dirent_read(cd->entry[i].orig, za->src, cd_buffer, false, error)) < 0) {\n        if (grown && zip_error_code_zip(error) == ZIP_ER_NOZIP) {\n            zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n        }\n        _zip_cdir_free(cd);\n        _zip_buffer_free(cd_buffer);\n        return NULL;\n    }\n    i++;\n    left -= (zip_uint64_t)entry_size;\n}\n```\n\nThe vulnerability is: `left -= (zip_uint64_t)entry_size` can underflow if `entry_size > left`. Since `left` is unsigned, the subtraction wraps around to a very large number, and the loop continues.\n\nBut wait - `entry_size` is the return value of `_zip_dirent_read`, which is `size + variable_size`. The `size` is `CDENTRYSIZE` (46) for central directory entries. The `variable_size` is `filename_len + ef_len + comment_len`, where each is a 16-bit value.\n\nSo `entry_size` is at least 46 and at most 46 + 65535 + 65535 + 65535 = 196651.\n\nIf `left` is less than `entry_size`, the subtraction underflows. For example, if `left` is 46 and `entry_size` is 100, then `left -= 100` gives `left = 46 - 100 = -54 = 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFCA` (as unsigned). The loop continues, trying to read more entries.\n\nBut `_zip_dirent_read` would fail because there's not enough data in `cd_buffer`. So the loop would exit with an error.\n\nWait, but what if `cd->size` is exactly the right size for the entries, but the entries have variable-size data that makes `entry_size` larger than expected? For example, if `cd->size` is 46 and there's one entry with `filename_len = 0`, `ef_len = 0`, `comment_len = 0`, then `entry_size = 46`. `left -= 46` gives `left = 0`. The loop exits. This is correct.\n\nBut what if `cd->size` is 46 and there's one entry with `filename_len = 10`, `ef_len = 0`, `comment_len = 0`? Then `entry_size = 56`. `left -= 56` gives `left = 46 - 56 = -10 = 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF6`. The loop continues.\n\nBut `_zip_dirent_read` would have read 56 bytes from `cd_buffer`, which only has 46 bytes. So `_zip_dirent_read` would have failed before returning.\n\nWait, let me re-read `_zip_dirent_read`:\n\n```c\nvariable_size = (zip_uint32_t)filename_len + (zip_uint32_t)ef_len + (zip_uint32_t)comment_len;\n\nif (from_buffer) {\n    if (_zip_buffer_left(buffer) < variable_size) {\n        zip_error_set(error, ZIP_ER_INCONS, 0);\n        return -1;\n    }\n}\n```\n\nThis checks if there's enough data in the buffer for the variable-size fields. But it checks `variable_size`, not `size + variable_size`. The `size` bytes (46 for central directory) were already read from the buffer before this check.\n\nSo if `cd_buffer` has 46 bytes, and the entry has `filename_len = 10`, then `variable_size = 10`. `_zip_buffer_left(buffer)` returns `46 - 46 = 0` (since the 46-byte header was already read). `0 < 10` is true, so the function returns an error. This is correct.\n\nBut wait - what if `cd_buffer` has 56 bytes (46 + 10), and the entry has `filename_len = 10`? Then `variable_size = 10`. `_zip_buffer_left(buffer)` returns `56 - 46 = 10`. `10 < 10` is false, so the check passes. The function reads the filename and returns `entry_size = 56`. `left -= 56` gives `left = 56 - 56 = 0`. The loop exits. This is correct.\n\nOK, so the underflow can only happen if `cd->size` is smaller than the actual size of the entries. But `cd->size` comes from the EOCD or EOCD64 record, and the entries are read from the central directory data. If `cd->size` is smaller than the actual size of the entries, `_zip_dirent_read` would fail because there's not enough data in `cd_buffer`.\n\nWait, but what if `cd->size` is larger than the actual size of the entries? Then `left` would be positive after reading all entries, and the check `left > 0` would fail. This is correct.\n\nWhat if `cd->size` is exactly the right size? Then `left` would be 0 after reading all entries. This is correct.\n\nSo the underflow can only happen if `cd->size` is smaller than the actual size of the entries. But `_zip_dirent_read` would fail in this case because there's not enough data in `cd_buffer`.\n\nUnless... `_zip_dirent_read` doesn't check if there's enough data for the variable-size fields when `from_buffer` is false. If the source doesn't have enough data, `_zip_buffer_new_from_source` would fail. This is correct.\n\nOK, I think the vulnerability might be in the `_zip_read_eocd64` function.    756	  private:
   757	
   758	  protected:
   759	  HBUINT16	coverageFormat;	/* Format identifier--format = 1 */
   760	  SortedArrayOf<GlyphID>
   761			glyphArray;	/* Array of GlyphIDs--in numerical order */
   762	  public:
   763	  DEFINE_SIZE_ARRAY (4, glyphArray);
   764	};
   765	
   766	struct CoverageFormat2
   767	{
   768	  friend struct Coverage;
   769	
   770	  private:
   771	  inline unsigned int get_coverage (hb_codepoint_t glyph_id) const
   772	  {
   773	    int i = rangeRecord.bsearch (glyph_id);
   774	    if (i != -1) {
   775	      const RangeRecord &range = rangeRecord[i];
   776	      return (unsigned int) range.value + (glyph_id - range.start);
   777	    }
   778	    return NOT_COVERED;
   779	  }
   780	
   781	  inline bool serialize (hb_serialize_context_t *c,
   782				 Supplier<GlyphID> &glyphs,
   783				 unsigned int num_glyphs)
   784	  {
   785	    TRACE_SERIALIZE (this);
   786	    if (unlikely (!c->extend_min (*this))) return_trace (false);
   787	
   788	    if (unlikely (!num_glyphs))
   789	    {
   790	      rangeRecord.len.set (0);
   791	      return_trace (true);
   792	    }
   793	
   794	    unsigned int num_ranges = 1;
   795	    for (unsigned int i = 1; i < num_glyphs; i++)
   796	      if (glyphs[i - 1] + 1 != glyphs[i])
   797	        num_ranges++;
   798	    rangeRecord.len.set (num_ranges);
   799	    if (unlikely (!c->extend (rangeRecord))) return_trace (false);
   800	
   801	    unsigned int range = 0;
   802	    rangeRecord[range].start = glyphs[0];
   803	    rangeRecord[range].value.set (0);
   804	    for (unsigned int i = 1; i < num_glyphs; i++)
   805	      if (glyphs[i - 1] + 1 != glyphs[i]) {
   806		range++;
   807		rangeRecord[range].start = glyphs[i];
   808		rangeRecord[range].value.set (i);
   809	        rangeRecord[range].end = glyphs[i];
   810	      } else {
   811	        rangeRecord[range].end = glyphs[i];
   812	      }
   813	    glyphs += num_glyphs;
   814	    return_trace (true);
   815	  }
   816	
   817	  inline bool sanitize (hb_sanitize_context_t *c) const
   818	  {
   819	    TRACE_SANITIZE (this);
   820	    return_trace (rangeRecord.sanitize (c));
   821	  }
   822	
   823	  inline bool intersects_coverage (const hb_set_t *glyphs, unsigned int index) const {
   824	    unsigned int i;
   825	    unsigned int count = rangeRecord.len;
   826	    for (i = 0; i < count; i++) {
   827	      const RangeRecord &range = rangeRecord[i];
   828	      if (range.value <= index &&
   829		  index < (unsigned int) range.value + (range.end - range.start) &&
   830		  range.intersects (glyphs))
   831	        return true;
   832	      else if (index < range.value)
   833	        return false;
   834	    }
   835	    return false;
   836	  }
   837	
   838	  template <typename set_t>
   839	  inline bool add_coverage (set_t *glyphs) const {
   840	    unsigned int count = rangeRecord.len;
   841	    for (unsigned int i = 0; i < count; i++)
   842	      if (unlikely (!rangeRecord[i].add_coverage (glyphs)))
   843	        return false;
   844	    return true;
   845	  }
   846	
   847	  public:
   848	  /* Older compilers need this to be public. */
   849	  struct Iter
   850	  {
   851	    inline void init (const CoverageFormat2 &c_)
   852	    {
   853	      c = &c_;
   854	      coverage = 0;
   855	      i = 0;
   856	      j = c->rangeRecord.len ? c->rangeRecord[0].start : 0;
   857	      if (unlikely (c->rangeRecord[0].start > c->rangeRecord[0].end))
   858	      {
   859	        /* Broken table. Skip. */
   860	        i = c->rangeRecord.len;
   861	      }
   862	    }
   863	    inline void fini (void) {};
   864	    inline bool more (void) { return i < c->rangeRecord.len; }
   865	    inline void next (void)
   866	    {
   867	      if (j >= c->rangeRecord[i].end)
   868	      {
   869	        i++;
   870		if (more ())
   871		{
   872		  hb_codepoint_t old = j;
   873		  j = c->rangeRecord[i].start;
   874		  if (unlikely (j <= old))
   875		  {
   876		    /* Broken table. Skip. Important to avoid DoS. */
   877		   i = c->rangeRecord.len;
   878		   return;
   879		  }
   880		  coverage = c->rangeRecord[i].value;
   881		}
   882		return;
   883	      }
   884	      coverage++;
   885	      j++;
   886	    }
   887	    inline hb_codepoint_t get_glyph (void) { return j; }
   888	    inline unsigned int get_coverage (void) { return coverage; }
   889	
   890	    private:

    74	                          const hb_set_t *unicodes,
    75	                          bool close_over_gsub,
    76	                          hb_set_t *unicodes_to_retain,
    77	                          hb_map_t *codepoint_to_glyph,
    78	                          hb_vector_t<hb_codepoint_t> *glyphs)

    84

    86	  initial_gids_to_retain->add (0); // Not-def
    87	
    88	  hb_codepoint_t cp = HB_SET_VALUE_INVALID;
    89	  while (unicodes->next (&cp))
    90	  {
    91	    hb_codepoint_t gid;
    92	    if (!cmap.get_nominal_glyph (cp, &gid))
    93	    {
    94	      DEBUG_MSG(SUBSET, nullptr, "Drop U+%04X; no gid", cp);
    95	      continue;
    96	    }
    97	    unicodes_to_retain->add (cp);
    98	    codepoint_to_glyph->set (cp, gid);
    99	    initial_gids_to_retain->add (gid);
   100	  }

Deterministic gates:
- Passed: source is not misanchored at the fuzzer harness; the saved MSan stack matches the general GSUB -> `page_for` -> `bfind` path; the top-level `source`, `root_cause`, and `sink` all point at existing `trace_step` entries.
- Failed: `static_valid`, `trace_complete`, `local_transitions_closed`, and `global_causal_chain_closed`.

Key blockers:
- The staged patch `c0a3f38f...` is an unrelated distcheck/Makefile change, so the GT’s claimed patch/root-cause relationship is unsupported.
- The root-cause narrative about zeroing `Coverage::Iter` is not established by the staged vulnerable source or the staged patch.
- The fine trace skips the constructor/materialization transition into `hb_auto_t<Coverage::Iter>`, which leaves the relevant initialization path open.
- The top-level sink line/code pair is inconsistent (`line 214` vs code from `line 211`).

Evidence limitations:
- Review was static only, using the staged metadata, `ground_truth.json`, saved sanitizer trace, local vulnerable-source snapshot, and patch/history inspection under `_work`.
- I did not run Docker, rebuild, rerun the PoC, or perform fixed-version validation.
